**Rev 3.31 changelog (one line), 2026-10-17:** §5.4's *Spill, don't truncate* gains a command's stub (plan-rev3 §5.4's N-TOOL-7, ZCode audit Z1-16): a spilled result of `shell_execute` or a `Command`-backend tool previews its head and tail at the `Err` path's budget — `"[result too large: <bytes> bytes; its head and tail follow]\n<head_tail(result, tool_result_inline_bytes)>\n[full result: result_ref=file:results/… — use read_result to page]"` (`session_log::command_spill_stub`) — while every other tool keeps the head-only stub verbatim; the `tool_result` record carries `stub {form: "head_tail", budget}` (`session_log::command_stub_marker`), so a session-log replay rebuilds the same stub from the spill file.

**Spine — three directives, all now decided:**

1. **Artifacts of all kinds live on disk under a `.openalpaca/` convention**, human-findable paths, DB stores only the address (rev 1's spine, kept).
2. **The app is not distributed.** Legacy-compatibility measures are **deleted from the code**, not merely avoided going forward (§3).
3. **New pillar: session persistence** — local persistence of session + project association + session recovery: SQLite tables + a per-session append-only JSONL event log + filesystem tiers (§5).

The `.openalpaca/` directory structure is designed as an **extensible contract** (§1): namespaces reserved deliberately, one Rust module owning every path, new content kinds added only through one enum.

**Verified against the tree** (not taken from any brief): migration head is **034** (`crates/openalpaca_storage/src/migrations/mod.rs`, tail `Migration { version: 34, name: "drop_context_compaction_log" }`); `unavailable.ts` holds exactly 23 gap ids; `walk_up_for_marker` prefers `.alpaca` then `.git` (`crates/openalpaca_core/src/memory/workspace.rs:43-56`); `workspace_id_from_root` returns the **canonical path string**, not a hash (`workspace.rs:60-65`); `app_dir()` = `directories::ProjectDirs::from("","","OpenAlpaca").data_dir()` and everything (`discovery_path`, `lock_path`, `database_path`, `assets_dir`) hangs off it (`crates/openalpaca_storage/src/paths.rs:10-83`); `conversations.lane_key` carries a **column-level `UNIQUE`** (`migrations/011_unified_conversations.sql:3-12`) — the single reason multi-conversation doesn't exist; `conversation_messages` is keyed by `lane_key`, not conversation id (`009_conversation_messages.sql`); the boot orphan sweep marks every non-terminal task `failed` (`apps/openalpacad/src/bootstrap/migration.rs:21-27` → `repository/task/mod.rs:187-202`); the agentic loop's transcript is memory-only (`runner/agentic_loop/mod.rs:205`, `LoopResult` returns no transcript — `runner/agentic_loop/config.rs:263-276`); `tool_execution_log` stores **no arguments and no results** (`030_skill_tool_execution_log.sql:29-40`); `chat.rs:462` is a literal `approval_scope: None`; `settings.rs:314` is a literal `daily_cost_usd = 0.0`; `list_orphaned` has no `origin` predicate (`repository/file_asset/mod.rs:95-112`); `total_storage_bytes` sums **all** rows (`mod.rs:76-85`); `SystemEvent::DagNodeStarted` is still produced at `runner/lead_agent/tools.rs:232-240`. The complete `paths.rs` consumer inventory and the session-side ground truth live in appendices R §0 and S §1 — every claim there carries a checked file:line.

## 0. Decisions (resolved)

Rev 1 §0 posed five either-way calls. All five are decided. The plan below is written against these; no alternatives are carried.

| # | Decision | Consequence the plan pays |
|---|---|---|
| **D1** | **`app_dir()` itself moves to `~/.openalpaca/`** — a single root for everything: DB, `discovery.json`, lock, master key, `plugins/`, upload assets, config, artifacts, sessions. | A live-DB relocation at boot. Paid cleanly by the one boot-time mover (§2.2): per-entry atomic rename, idempotent resume, live-daemon guard, WAL sidecars before the DB file, abort-on-first-failure — ~80 lines replacing `migrate_legacy_app_dir()` in the same call slot. Plus an **atomic three-binary rebuild** (daemon + CLI + GUI in one commit); no compatibility window exists and none is designed. |
| **D2** | **Chat/connector uploads also move into `.openalpaca/`** — human-named under `uploads/`, not content-addressed. | New uploads write via `upload_dir()`/`upload_file_name()`; dedup keys off the existing owner-scoped `sha256` DB query (`routes/files.rs:172-185`), not the path. Existing content-addressed blobs park at `state/assets/` (mover) with a boot-time `storage_path` prefix UPDATE; the full re-home into `uploads/` is a Phase 8 item (§6, Phase 8.3). The connector's duplicated sha/dedup write path (`connectors/src/common/mod.rs:213-262`) collapses into the single writer first. |
| **D3** | **`extracted_text` stays in the DB** — a derived text index, bounded at 50 000 chars (`daemon_config/upload.rs:28`), on the prompt-assembly hot path. | Nothing; this was already the schema in §4.4. |
| **D4** | **`~/.openalpaca/`**, with an **`OPENALPACA_HOME_STORE`** env override (absolute paths only — a relative value is rejected; it would re-introduce CWD-dependence). | One resolution function, `home_root()` (§2.1). |
| **D5** | **`start` keeps the same task id** (200). | `dispatch_lead_agent_with_id` + idempotent `TaskRepository::upsert_queued` — acknowledged as the least clean code in the plan; isolated and named in review (§6 Phase 5, risk table §9). |

Two calls carried from rev 1, still standing:

- **`daily_cost_usd` comes from the DB** (`query_daily_usage` for today), not `CostTracker::total_cost()` — the tracker means *since boot*.
- **`calls_7d` is renamed `messages_7d`** (GAP-17). The only measurable number is inbound user messages by `conversation_messages.source`.

### New calls raised by rev 2 — **N1–N5 all resolved.** N1–N3 took their default (2026-09-01); N4 is a product call answered by the owner (2026-09-01); N5's *model* was fixed 2026-09-01 and its *mechanism* accepted 2026-09-02 as `tasks/extension-enable-design.md` rev 15 (verified 2026-09-02) — implement N5 from that document, never from this table.

| # | Question | Decision |
|---|---|---|
| **N1** | **Connector session boundaries.** Telegram/iMessage lanes get one perpetual `active` session. Is an idle-auto-archive (e.g. 72 h) wanted, and/or an in-chat `/new` command? The config knob (`[orchestrator.sessions] idle_archive_hours`) is reserved either way. | **Perpetual session.** Knob reserved (`idle_archive_hours`), off. |
| **N2** | **`GET /v1/status.log_path`.** Rev 1 shipped `null` unconditionally (the daemon writes no log). After the move, the *CLI-managed* start pipes daemon output to `state/logs/daemon.log` (`apps/openalpaca/src/manager.rs:38`). Serve that path when the file exists, or keep `null` until GAP-14 Phase B? | **Serve it when present**; `null` for GUI-sidecar daemons. |
| **N3** | **Early S0.** If the GUI wants multi-conversation before the artifact Library completes, sessions Phase 7a can be pulled ahead of Phase 3 — its only hard prerequisite is Phase 6's `#[derive(Default)]` churn payment, which can be paid early. | **Ship in the §6 order.** |
| **N4** | **Cost-cap semantics.** `max_cost` is enforced per-workflow (`lead_agent_defaults` $5) and per-agent-turn (`agent_defaults` $1), but the Settings panel presents today's spend as if against a *daily* budget. Add a real daily cap, or relabel? | **Per-workflow — relabel; no daily cap is added.** `/v1/usage/summary.caps` carries `workflow_max_cost_usd` / `agent_max_cost_usd` and **no** `daily_*` key; today's spend is reported as an informational total with no denominator, so the design's progress bar stays omitted (it was never drawn). A daily budget would be a new enforcement point in the LLM router, not a label — out of scope. **Corollary:** the main-loop cost lockout (`tasks/bug-main-loop-cost-lockout.md`) is confirmed a bug, not a daily budget working as intended. **Fix shape for the corollary (P-3 / C-3, adopted):** option 1 of the bug note — baseline `state.last_cost` from `agent_cost()` before round 0 so the $1 budget is per turn again; no daily budget, no attribution-row change. **What "per-workflow" measures today (P-2a, verified — recorded, no enforcement change):** subagent spawns pass the parent `task_id` into the loop (`runner/lead_agent/tools.rs:592`), so `task_id`-keyed *reporting* (`get_task_usage`/`cost_for_tasks`) already includes subagent spend; but `agent_cost()` prefers the agent-scoped bucket over the task bucket (`runner/agentic_loop/backend.rs:167-189`) and every subagent starts a fresh accumulator, so the lead's $5 `max_cost` bounds only the lead's own calls. Pre-check (d) below measures exactly this. Charging subagent spend to the lead's accumulator would be a **new enforcement point** (workflows die sooner; a subagent dollar counts against its own $1 turn cap and the lead's $5) and is therefore **owner decision T13 — pending, not a default** (see "Owner decisions pending"). *Taken 2026-10-17 (PDT; 2026-10-18T01:56Z) by the owner's blanket answer as the relabel — no workflow accumulator, this row stays relabel-only, N-LEAD-1 first; not built (§0's twelfth* Owner decisions taken *subsection, T13).* Precedent, for the record only: Claude Code's `/cost` is per session and every daily/monthly limit lives outside the tool; its one hard cap (`--max-budget-usd`) does count subagents. |
| **N5** | **Tool allow / enable model.** What governs which tools an agent gets, and what the GUI toggles? | **RESOLVED — model + semantics settled 2026-09-01; mechanism accepted 2026-09-02 as `tasks/extension-enable-design.md` rev 15 (verified 2026-09-02)** (design of record; ADR-030 supersedes ADR-029). Two axes: **allow** is per-agent (template `capabilities`, skills' `requires_capabilities` — exists today); **enable** is per-extension. Settled: **(S1)** the toggle sits on **each MCP server and each plugin** — *not* per tool; the install unit is the toggle unit. **(S2)** *disabled* means **unloaded** — kill the plugin child process, drop the MCP connection. A lifecycle state, not a display filter; a disabled extension runs nothing and must not attempt reconnect. **(S3)** extensions carry distinct states — **Disabled**, **Unapproved** (approval gate not passed), **Crashed** (internal error: won't run, can't connect, or needs authorization). **(S4)** withdrawn capabilities: the agent **cannot** use the tool **and a warning is emitted**, surfacing in the log and/or chat — silent degradation is rejected. **Builtins are never toggled**; agent config alone governs them. **Mechanism, in two lines:** the owner's bit is persisted *write-first* in the declaration itself (`mcp.toml` `[servers.<n>] enabled`; `.permissions.toml` `enabled` beside a tri-state consent) while an in-memory `ExtensionLedger` inside `ToolRegistry` holds observed state (`Enabled \| Disabled \| Unapproved{reason} \| Failed{reason, detail, since} \| Orphaned`, transient `Enabling/Disabling`; *needs authorization* is a `FailureReason` with an `actionable` bit, not a state) stamped with a per-load `generation`. Disable = W persist → T0 gate → T1 deregister → T2 withdraw contributed skills/templates/providers → T3 drain → T4 kill/disconnect (the MCP client's `closed` seal is checked at `reconnect`'s entry **and** at `do_handshake`'s install point) → T5 commit; enable = W → E0 CAS (a no-op on `Enabled`, never a reload) → E1 consent + drift check → E2–E5 load; `reload` is the third verb; crashes reach `Failed{Crashed}` through a generation-carrying reaper; a connected server that changes its tool set mid-session (`notifications/tools/list_changed`, design §3.7) is refreshed in place under the mutex only while `Enabled` at the notifying generation — removed names withdrawn through T1 with attribution and kept flagged `server_withdrawn`, added names admitted through E4, no generation bump; S4 fires at three moments (attempted use, withdrawal at declaration, the transition) under design §7.5's log-versus-chat rule. The design's owner-gated refinements (§13 Q5–Q14) are **not applied** there or here. *Since 2026-10-17 (PDT; 2026-10-18T01:56Z) Q5–Q12 and Q14 are taken as their recommendations by the owner's blanket answer, none built, Q8's sub-choice pending, and Q13 (T14) is taken as its recommendation, moot, with nothing to build (§0's twelfth* Owner decisions taken *subsection).* |

Four **implementer pre-checks** (verify, not decide):

- **(a)** bundled SQLite ≥ 3.35 for `ALTER TABLE … DROP COLUMN` in migration 035 — else use the 024-style table rebuild.
- **(b)** before deleting the legacy flat `llm.toml` branch (P4), confirm `LlmConfig` and `build_provider_with_runtime` have no non-legacy consumers.
- **(c) Keychain `secret_ref` naming per root (P-1/T10).** Verify how `secret_ref` keychain accounts are named today. If they are keyed per provider/key id only (not root-scoped), a second `OPENALPACA_HOME_STORE` root — a test root, say — silently shares the real keychain secrets. **The id source, so the check is implementable:** `ensure_store()` writes a second line `install_id=<uuid-v4>` into the **home** root's `.layout` on first creation and never rewrites it (§1.1); the project root's `.layout` carries a separate `project_id` (§1.2, P-12); both files are parsed as `key=value` lines after line 1. Whether accounts are then root-scoped (`openalpaca/<install_id>/<provider>/<key_id>`) or sharing is accepted is **owner decision T10 — pending** (*taken 2026-10-17 (PDT; 2026-10-18T01:56Z) by the owner's blanket answer: accounts root-scoped by `install_id`; not built — the twelfth* Owner decisions taken *subsection*); the pre-check establishes the fact and lands the id, nothing more. Not a change to D4.
- **(d) Does the per-workflow cost cap count subagent spend? (P-2a/T13) — a MEASUREMENT, not a change.** Run one two-subagent workflow; compare `get_task_usage(task_id)` (task-wide reporting) against the accumulator `agent_cost()` reads for the lead at `runner/agentic_loop/backend.rs:167-189`; write both numbers into the N4 row. The expected result (task-wide reporting, lead-only cap) is already stated there. No enforcement point is added or moved on the strength of this check — that is T13. *T13 was taken 2026-10-17 (PDT; 2026-10-18T01:56Z) as the relabel, so none is; the measurement was never run (no provider keys).*

### Owner decisions pending — none since 2026-10-19 (PDT; 2026-10-20T00:26Z); one, T6(a)'s sub-choice, from 2026-10-17 until then

**Since the owner's answer of 2026-10-19 (PDT; 2026-10-20T00:26Z) this table holds no pending row.** Its one row, T6(a)'s sub-choice, is taken — a third option, the ledger learning `Failed` at a stdio child's exit — and recorded in the thirteenth *Owner decisions taken* subsection below; the row stays, its recommendation cell opening with the stamp and its earlier text kept after *As recorded:*. No decision of this plan's rows waits on the owner since (B-09's row still waits on MD-4's later design, `tasks/pending-decisions.md` §4.6; that file's rev 9 §1 lists everything that still waits). *As recorded at rev 3.30:* **Since the owner's blanket answer of 2026-10-17 (PDT; 2026-10-18T01:56Z) this table holds one row.** Every row it held was taken as its recommendation — the ZCode audit's where it gave or changed one (T13, T18, T22, T24, T35), otherwise the lessons' or the source's — and moved to the twelfth *Owner decisions taken* subsection below with the option and where its work is planned; none is built, and the shipped code stays the "no change" answer until a commit builds one. T14 and T31, moot in fact, are taken there as their recommendations, with nothing to build. The rows' full text as they stood is rev 3.29 of this file (`git show 476f1f81:tasks/api-fix-plan.md`) and `tasks/pending-decisions.md` (rev 6). What stays pending is the one sub-choice below, which has no recommendation recorded.

*The record of who raised each row, as rev 3.29 wrote it:* Raised by `tasks/research/claude-code-design-lessons.md` §6 against rules that are settled. Each carries the lessons' recommended default; **the default is a recommendation, not a decision, and this plan is written as if the answer were "no change" until the owner says otherwise.** Design-side twins (T1, T3, T4, T6(a), T6(c), T7, T8, T9, T14, T15) are also listed as `tasks/extension-enable-design.md` §13 Q5–Q14 and are unapplied there too. **T16–T22 were raised during execution**, not by the lessons — T16 and T17 by ruling R40 and the uninstall verb of Phase 8 item 9, T18 and T19 by the whole-branch review (ruling R86: a finding refuted as owner-decision territory is recorded, never adopted), T20 and T21 by the local-model readiness wave (rulings L1–L14), whose spec recorded both as decisions rather than shipping them, and T22 by that wave's round 4 (ruling V10) against the route round 3 added. **T24** was raised by the GUI first-run track (D-F/D-G), which wired the key routes but deliberately not the credential-discovery pair; it is T24 rather than T23 because T23 belongs to the daemon-lifecycle track. **T25–T27 and T31–T33** were raised by the simplification wave's integrated plan (the factory-reset, legacy-mover and daemon-lifecycle tracks) and shipped their no-change answer; that wave's T23, T28, T29 and T30 are not in this table because they are decided — see *Owner decisions taken* below it. **LQ5–LQ10**, raised while building Learning v2 on `feat/markdown-memory` and listed in full in `tasks/memory-redesign/09-learning-design.md` §10, were pointer rows here until the owner took them on 2026-09-28; they are under *Owner decisions taken* below now (rev 3.15). **T34 and FW-2** were raised by Round G (2026-10-03, rev 3.20) — T34 by its live check, FW-2 by the re-test that led to FW-1 — and shipped their no-change answer; the storage design took both on 2026-10-05 (rev 3.21): T34 is under *Owner decisions taken* below, built as its Stage 0's F1, and FW-2's row stayed here, rewritten, until its Stage 2 built it (`cfebde4c..d673478e`, rev 3.24): it is under *Owner decisions taken* below now. **T35** was raised by the storage design's panel (`tasks/storage-design/design.md` §0.3, §18) and is not built. **T36** was raised while Stage 1 of that design was built (rev 3.22) and answered by the owner on 2026-10-09 with no change; it moved to the storage design's *Owner decisions taken* subsection, beside **ST-23** (rev 3.23). **T37** was raised by that design's Stage 2 live check (rev 3.25) and is not built.

| # | Question | Recommendation | What each answer changes |
|---|---|---|---|
| **T6(a)'s sub-choice** (`tasks/extension-enable-design.md` §13 Q8; T6(a) itself taken 2026-10-17 (PDT; 2026-10-18T01:56Z) as (1) — the twelfth subsection below) | Under T6(a) (1), automatic recovery of a crashed stdio MCP child in the fix N-MCP-1's form: when does the extension ledger learn `Failed` for a server that keeps failing — **(s1)** after the respawns, consecutive failed handshakes counting toward `ReconnectExhausted`, as N-MCP-1 is written; or **(s2)** at the next call? | **Taken 2026-10-19 (PDT; 2026-10-20T00:26Z)**: neither (s1) nor (s2) — a third option, **the ledger learns at the child's exit**: when a stdio server's child exits, its row reads `Failed` at once, its detail the exit code or signal, and `extension_state_changed` is emitted; recovery as T6(a) (1) took it, the reconnect-once returning the row to `Enabled` on success *(decided by: Claude Code (secondhand), the audit's T6 row, and ZCode's code, `ZCode:apps/zcode-cli/packages/adapters/src/mcp/index.ts:1619-1629`)*; not built; scheduled at N-MCP-1 (amended), its ledger half, in its place (the thirteenth subsection below). *As recorded:* None recorded — the owner's; it touches ext design §3.6 item 1. ### Owner decisions taken — `chore/deps-upgrade-and-simplify`, 2026-09-22

The owner settled the simplification wave's scope as **D-A…D-G** (D-A a factory reset deletes the database file and its `-wal`/`-shm`; D-B its confirmation names the store root and what survives, and takes a typed word; D-C it refuses while a daemon runs; D-D the legacy disk-layout movers are retired for a boot diagnostic; D-E the daemon's two lifecycle defects are fixed before the GUI control; D-F the GUI key form refuses a switched-off provider; D-G "setup completed" is derived from `llm.effective_default_model !== null`). Four questions that wave raised are decided too. Each row names the commit that applied it; unlike the table above, these **are** the shipped behaviour.

| # | Question | Decision | Applied by |
|---|---|---|---|
| **T23** | Should `GET /v1/status` carry a `busy` block so a client can warn honestly before stopping the daemon? | **Adopted (owner).** A counts-only block, `busy: { running_tasks, pending_confirmations, connected_clients }` — one `COUNT(*)` over the non-terminal task rows (`TaskRepository::count_non_terminal`) and two in-memory reads (the confirmation broker's pending count, `EventBroadcaster::client_count`); `null` on a daemon with no broker, never a guessed zero; never the prompts' contents. It widens what `/v1/status` is *for* (the installation → also its workload), which is why it was a decision, and it answers the stop dialog without leaning on the unscoped reads T16 and T22 are about. | `743817ad` (`apps/openalpacad/src/routes/status.rs`); read by the GUI's stop dialog, `1d08365d` |
| **T28** | With the mover gone, a database at **both** the legacy root and `~/.openalpaca` no longer has to be chosen between. Keep the downgrade from a refused boot to one `WARN`? | **Adopted (owner).** Keep the `WARN` (`LegacyRoot::Residue { holds_database: true }`). The old refusal existed because the mover had to pick which database to boot on; with no mover there is no choice and nothing merges them. The **stranded** case still refuses: critical data at the legacy root and no database here means starting would create an empty one beside it. One line in `classify_legacy_root` reverses it. **Moot since SQ-2 (a) (2026-09-28):** the store revision's S3 deletes `store/legacy_root.rs` whole, so no older data directory is classified, warned about or refused over any more. | `a0383e3e` (`crates/openalpaca_storage/src/store/legacy_root.rs`) |
| **T29** | `sysinfo` enters `openalpaca_storage`, making the leaf storage crate process-aware (the natural home, `openalpaca_platform`, was an unused scaffold deleted at `1bad6352`). | **Adopted by the commit that needed it.** One liveness predicate in the tree — `daemon_lifecycle::pid_is_daemon` — shared by the CLI, the factory reset's daemon check and the GUI shell, rather than a fourth crate for three functions or a copy in each binary. `sysinfo` was already a workspace dependency; `Cargo.lock` gained one edge and no package. | `26085722` (`crates/openalpaca_storage/src/daemon_lifecycle.rs`) |
| **T30** | Once the GUI sidecar writes `state/logs/daemon.log`, `GET /v1/status.log_path` is non-null for a sidecar-launched daemon. Widen `managed_log` from "`openalpaca daemon start` marked this run" to "whichever launcher pointed stdio at the file and rotated it", or add a second flag? | **Adopted by the commit that needed it.** Widen the meaning — the flag was always about *ownership* of the file, not which launcher; the doc comments, the Connection panel's note and the manuals changed in the same commit. `log_path` is `null` only for a daemon started by hand. | `79e3cc98` (`apps/openalpacad/src/state.rs`, `store::MANAGED_LOG_ENV`) |

### Owner decisions taken — `feat/markdown-memory`, 2026-09-23 (the Markdown memory redesign)

The owner answered the memory redesign's nine questions on 2026-09-23 (`tasks/memory-redesign/03-design.md` §0; seven followed the recommendation, MD-6 and MD-9 did not) and three decisions its audit raised (G2, G30, G38), and instructed that the old SQL/vector memory engine be **removed completely, with no compatibility code** — no conversion, no leftover-key warnings, no legacy-cache cleanup. Each row names the commits that applied it; like the table above, these **are** the shipped behaviour. The design's `§` references are to rev 4.2, the plan's to `04-implementation-plan.md` rev 4.2.

| # | Question | Decision | Applied by |
|---|---|---|---|
| **MD-1** | Retire embeddings and `sqlite-vec`, with no optional semantic path in v1? | **Adopted (owner, the recommendation).** Memory search is lexical only — FTS5 `unicode61` plus a trigram companion in a disposable cache file — and the embedder, its model download, `sqlite-vec`, the `local-embeddings` feature and every embedding key are gone. | `d4be1e54` (the engine, `ensure_vec_extension`, the `vec0` table), `ed1b659f` (`sqlite-vec`, `fastembed`, `local-embeddings`, the config keys) |
| **MD-2** | What does automatic learning produce? | **Adopted (owner, the recommendation): by source role.** What the user said in their own words becomes an **active** note with origin `extracted_user`, listed as *recently learned* with one-step undo; what was inferred from assistant or tool output becomes a **candidate**, outside recall until confirmed. The input is the message as typed (`HandleRequest.content`), never an attachment's text; at most `extract_max_items_per_turn` (5) notes per turn and `task_extract_max_items_per_run` (10) per run; a failed or cancelled run produces nothing. | `2ef130fd` (the count keys), `c4d46f94` (chat extraction), `1db33d8c` (task-output candidates), `f82d4f89` (undo), `1d78d1f1` (`recently-learned`), `7a900120` (the GUI list and its undo) |
| **MD-3** | What happens to the background `USER.md` writer? | **Adopted (owner, the recommendation).** It stops writing learned facts into `USER.md`; an explicit editor ships in the same slice (`GET\|PUT /v1/persona/user` with `If-Match`, `openalpaca persona show\|edit user`, Settings → Persona); onboarding completes through `update_persona` with no background writer. | `c4d46f94` (the writer stops), `2e8b9c92` (the routes), `f9d22ade` (the CLI), `2a25b47a` (`If-Match: *`), `de37e342` (Settings → Persona) |
| **MD-4** | Block recall in group chats? | **Adopted (owner, the recommendation): v1 keeps today's lane model.** No group lane exists to block; group privacy is its own *connector audience* design, covering `USER.md`, transcript, notices and memory together. | No code of its own: `8fabf9e0`'s per-surface construction gives a connector lane the linked owner, and an unlinked identity no owner at all |
| **MD-5** | Which workspace scopes memory? | **Adopted (owner, the recommendation).** The project partition is the marker root (`.openalpaca`, else `.git`) of the request's workspace, created lazily on the first project write; a request with no marker is global; the daemon's own CWD never selects a project; every path component to the partition is checked for links. Extends ADR-031. | `8fabf9e0` (`MemoryAccessContext`, partition resolution), `b774e3c6` (recall through the service), `a7b9e94a` (the workspace move re-keys the partition) |
| **MD-6** | What happens to existing schema-42 memories? | **Refuse and reset (owner; the recommendation was a converting migration 043).** The baseline is re-squashed to **43** without memory tables; a schema-42 database is refused before any statement runs, with a message naming `openalpaca config reset --factory`, everything it removes and what survives; nothing is converted. *(Since SQ-1, 2026-09-28 — below: the baseline registers 1, and a 42 database meets the one refusal of a version above this build's, whose text names the reset and the switch-over note; nothing is converted still.)* | `2f500ea1` (the schema-42 fixture), `d4be1e54` (the re-squash and the 42 refusal), `a7b9e94a` (the factory-reset preview), `6ad6313a` (the switch-over note in `docs/Installation_Manual.md`) |
| **MD-7** | Is project memory git-ignored? | **Adopted (owner, the recommendation).** By a self-contained `.gitignore` (`*`) inside `<project>/.openalpaca/memory/`, rewritten whenever its bytes differ, on every partition open; the user's own ignore rules are never edited. | `6c2a6998` and `0d96af48` (the text and its assertion), `a7b9e94a` (the project README row) |
| **MD-8** | Fix the current engine's data loss before the replacement lands? | **Adopted (owner, the recommendation), as slice 0.** No decay pruning; no similarity supersession for any caller; the lead reads global only without a workspace; `config reset` keeps `identity.*`; a database newer than the build is refused naming no remedy. | `887ad72a`, `0b5205ec`, `0a6e8f73`, `e8e56a6a`, `ae8faca8` |
| **MD-9** | Which management surfaces ship with the cutover? | **Everything at once (owner; the recommendation was API and CLI first).** The `/v1/memories*` routes, `openalpaca memory …`, and the GUI Memory view with Settings → Persona, all in slice 3. | `1d78d1f1` (routes), `b35fda54` (CLI), `bda825b9`, `7a900120`, `de37e342`, `ea4efefb` (GUI) |
| **G2** | May a model tool call mint origin `user`? | **No (owner).** Only the API, the CLI and the GUI write origin `user`; a tool call records `extracted_user`, `confirm` is refused by the tool naming the Memory view and `openalpaca memory confirm`, and a tool-issued edit, archive or supersede of a `user` note is `authority_conflict`. *(Rev 3.12: since **LQ11**, 2026-09-26 — the table after the next — a tool-issued supersede, or an edit that changes a saved note's content, is written as a suggestion the user confirms instead; archive of a `user` note is still `authority_conflict`, and a tool call still never mints `user`.)* | `f82d4f89` (the service rules), `dfb9a3f6` (the tools) |
| **G30** | Where does the Memory view live, and how does it stay fresh? | **A fifth top-level view** after Library, and a `memory_changed` `ServerEvent` persisted nowhere, which the view invalidates on instead of polling (owner). | `15bbcee9` (the event and its bridge arms), `7a900120` (the view) |
| **G38** | Inventory the facts the old writer put into `USER.md`? | **No (owner).** Nothing lists or moves them; the switch-over note tells the owner to review `USER.md` by hand. | No code: `6ad6313a` (step 6 of the switch-over note) |

### Owner decisions taken — `feat/markdown-memory`, 2026-09-24 (Learning v2)

After slice 3 the owner made automatic learning the priority and decided **MD-10…MD-16** (design 03 §0, *Learning v2*; all as recommended) and answered the four questions of the Learning v2 design (`tasks/memory-redesign/09-learning-design.md` §10): **LQ1** against the recommendation, **LQ2–LQ4** as recommended. Each row names the commits that applied it; like the tables above, these **are** the shipped behaviour. The `§` references are to 09 rev 3.22; every slice's deviations are its revision-log entries.

| # | Question | Decision | Applied by |
|---|---|---|---|
| **MD-10** | When does learning run, and on what? | **Adopted (owner, the recommendation).** Every turn is queued; a session's turns since the last run are extracted together when it has been idle about 2 minutes (`[orchestrator.learning] idle_secs`, 120), when 10 of the user's turns have accumulated (`turns_per_flush`), on a conversation switch, and in a 3 s stop drain whose durable half is a boot catch-up; nothing is skipped or cut to 800/400, and a reply never waits. The cursor is the `learning_cursor` table (migration 044; in the baseline since SQ-1); daily caps are durable (`extract_max_calls_per_day` 200, `extract_max_items_per_call` 12). | L1 `48f50793..f00bbc9a` (migration 044, the keys, the queue, the flush, the triggers and caps, and the review's seven fixes) |
| **MD-11** | When learning finds a replacement, is it applied? | **Adopted (owner, the recommendation).** Automatic when the older note was itself learned (*recently learned* with undo, which withdraws); a **suggestion** the user confirms when the user saved, edited or imported it — nothing learned silently overrides a user-authored note (G2). Learned notes carry aliases and a `fact_key`. | L2 `33bf9226..cfb05abf` (index schema 2, the known-notes lookup, aliases, keys, the change test); L3 `9e533a66..3f356e56` (suggestions, the bound confirm, withdrawals, the route, the CLI, the GUI's Suggestions card) |
| **MD-12** | Can a suggestion be confirmed in chat? | **Adopted (owner, the recommendation).** The assistant raises it with `memory_confirm` on an attended app/CLI turn and the person answers on the ordinary Approve/Deny card, which shows a daemon-written preview; only the Approve for that call confirms (per call, never cached or auto-approved); the Memory view and the CLI keep it too. | L4 `63b29ba9..d347c246` |
| **MD-13** | Chat memory commands? | **Adopted (owner).** `/remember`, `/forget`, `/memories`, answered by the daemon with no model on attended app/CLI turns; `/remember` records origin `user`. | L9 `15318ddd..03c253d4` |
| **MD-14** | Layered memory tools? | **Adopted (owner).** `memory_search` answers an index by default, with date filters and `order_by`; `memory_read` takes up to 10 ids; `memory_timeline` is new (`GET /v1/memories/timeline`, `openalpaca memory timeline`). Index schema 3. | L10a `b71d6e9d..a90202ed` |
| **MD-15** | Index-first recall each turn? | **Adopted (owner).** `[orchestrator.memory] recall_mode = "index"` (default): pinned notes, the top 2 as excerpts and up to 8 index lines; `excerpts` keeps the old delivery; the evaluation measures both. | L10b `19f46c61..3db79ce1` |
| **MD-16** | Conversation summaries? | **Adopted (owner).** An `episode` note per stretch of at least 4 user turns, after 30 minutes' quiet, a switch or the stop drain; findable by search and timeline, never recalled automatically, forgotten with its conversation (migration 045 — in the baseline since SQ-1 — and `episode_max_per_day` 20, *100 since LQ21, 2026-09-28*). | L10c `52668aca..fa09dc0c` |
| **LQ1** | Which judge grades the learning evaluation? | **The same model judges, marked as such (owner; the recommendation was a cloud judge of another vendor).** The judge defaults to the reader's model, the guard refuses only an unroutable judge, and the report records `judge_is_reader`, `judge_same_family` and `judge_is_extractor` (09 §7.4, rewritten at 09 rev 3). | L7 `b881b527` (the harness and its judge guard) |
| **LQ2** | What does **Deny** on the in-chat card do? | **Dismiss it for good (owner, the recommendation):** a withdrawal of the candidate, the same act as *Keep mine* — no links, no suppressed source. | L4 `d27c034a` (`BuiltInTool::on_confirmation_denied`, `MemoryService::dismiss_suggestion`) |
| **LQ3** | `memory_read` for every agent? | **Yes, for internal templates, by an ambient grant (owner, the recommendation);** all nine shipped templates name it; a plugin-contributed template gets it only by naming it; `denied_capabilities` wins. | L5 `eb98b820` (`AgentTemplate::to_subagent`, the three templates) |
| **LQ4** | What happens to the unlearned turns of a deleted or cleared conversation? | **Dropped (owner, the recommendation).** `DELETE /v1/sessions/{id}`, `DELETE /v1/chat/history` and the workspace purge drop them; a flush that finds its session gone writes nothing; since L10c the same three forget the conversation's episode notes. | L1 `9a5b0507`, `1a21190e`, `92e9063f` (the route hooks); L10c `bc673ad2` (the summaries) |

### Owner decisions taken — `feat/markdown-memory`, 2026-09-26 (the L8 evaluation)

The first runs of Learning v2's evaluation, L8 (2026-09-25…26; `qwen3.8:27b-mlx` as reader, extractor and judge, so every figure is the reader grading itself), were diagnosed — five findings, each checked by two sceptics — and the benches were profiled. Together they raised seven questions, which the owner decided on 2026-09-26 in two batches: **LQ11**, **LQ12** and **LQ14** from the diagnosis, with **LQ13** deferred until a capture run recorded the unusable answers' exact form; then **LQ13**, **LQ15**, **LQ16** and **LQ17**, with the capture and the scale analysis. `tasks/memory-redesign/09-learning-design.md` §10 lists them with their evidence (the session scratchpad's `memeval/l8/diagnosis/`, `capture/` and `bench/scale/`, not tracked), its revision log (revs 3.26–3.44) records how each was built, and `03-design.md` folds the contract changes (revs 7.4–7.15). Each row names the commits that applied it; like the tables above, these **are** the shipped behaviour. One ruling after them is the lead's, not the owner's: **tq-0032**, the one row of the retrieval proxy's eight totals that LQ17's layout loses (index tuning, listed or delivered, 95 → 94), was accepted on 2026-09-27 because the held-out gate set shows no net loss and every variant that recovered the row cost the held-out set at least one (09 §6.5.5).

| # | Question | Decision | Applied by |
|---|---|---|---|
| **LQ11** | The chat model's `memory_store` naming a note the user saved in `supersedes` was refused `authority_conflict` (09 §3.1 row 5, under G2), after which the model stored the new value as a second, contradicting active note, and no suggestion was raised (L-S: 4 of 8 missing). Keep the refusal? | **Reversed (owner): the chat model's replacement of a note the user saved is a suggestion** the user confirms, through learning's path — nothing active changes until then, and G2 holds (no `user` origin minted, nothing retired, no confirm by a tool call). The lead's reading, built: a store under such a note's `fact_key` and a `memory_update edit` that changes its title, body, aliases, tags, key or validity are the same replacement; origin `user`, `external` or `imported` all count as saved; archive of a `user` note stays refused, and forget is unchanged. The close-out added: a pending candidate's forget suppresses no row the note it names, or any saved note, cites; a forget takes the named suggestions first, and its result does not depend on target order; a suggestion lists what else it changes (`changes`, an addition to 03 §7a). | `9f1bb2f3`, `abdd9645`, `79f00f49` (product slice P1); `edb62bbb` (its repair round 1); `dffe7413`, `1a6a69b8` (round 2); `f928d25e`, `17ba8538`, `46d5008c`, `9ab4898d` (the close-out); `d515613f`, `941ac190`, `beb5efcb`, `92ec9799`, `c3fb1cf8` (its repair round 1); `91fe6bef`, `d9840624`, `b2b877d2` (round 2); `3f3a2307`, `2b49639e` (round 3) |
| **LQ12** | The learning metrics counted the reader's own `memory_store` notes as learned, so a live run and the extraction-only replays measured two different systems. | **Report every learning metric three ways — extraction, model tool and pooled (owner); the §7.3 targets are read against extraction; QA accuracy uses the whole store.** | `bf094ca8` (H3, the metrics by surface) and `c0349703` (H4, a replay labelled extraction-only), in the L8 harness slice |
| **LQ13** | About one extraction answer in six was unusable in the first L8 runs (45 single-call flushes stopped on one, of 274, plus 4 of 5 forced re-runs; [10](memory-redesign/10-learning-evaluation.md) §6 counts them), the same way every time at temperature 0. | **Decide after a capture run records the failing answers' exact form (owner, first batch). Then, with the capture — 9 of its 11 unusable answers a bare array of valid items, 1 followed by `</think>`, 1 after prose — tolerant unwrap (owner, second batch):** `<think>` blocks and prose around the answer stripped, the one JSON value it carries taken (the caller's shape preferred), a bare items array read as the items; the schema checks unchanged, every item still validated, and a truncated answer never rescued (*since LQ20 (f), 2026-09-28, `20c6d9df`: chat learning's extraction reads a cut answer that ends on a complete value; conversation summaries and task extraction still never do*). | `6120812b`; `f4a1e5e6` (repair round 1); `04df5bce` (round 2) |
| **LQ14** | The extractor is told to reuse known keys, and the reader's coarse or value-bearing keys (`editor.preference`, `user.machine`) led it to replace or hide unrelated facts. | **Keys only the chat model coined are not offered to the extractor (owner)** — not as known keys, key targets or on a known note's line; the notes stay searchable, recallable and listed, and a key learning or the user also holds is still offered. The datum is origin `extracted_user` with no `observed_at` (`memory_index::LEARNING_KEY_HOLDER`); no snapshot field was added. | `0fe524d8`; `2d333522` (repair round 1) |
| **LQ15** | Which store size does the memory service's cost target? The benches ran to 100K notes; a heavy user reaches about 4–10K notes a year and a very heavy one 13–32K. | **About 30K notes now, 100K later (owner).** | No code — a target, read by LQ17's measurements and the benches |
| **LQ16** | Drop ruling R3's stat scan before every read (option O4, or a watcher), or keep it? | **Keep R3 and make the scan cheaper (owner):** one `getattrlistbulk(2)` pass over `notes/` on macOS, `readdir` + `lstat` elsewhere, the same entries and fields bit for bit; 221–233 → 99–108 ms per 100,000-file scan, release. | `0177a41f`; `5f36b1dd` (repair round 1) |
| **LQ17** | At 100K notes index search + verification was p95 150 ms, over 09 §6.4's 100 ms rule, and the cache 1,065 MiB, half of it trigram data. | **Write, and build, the index-layout revision (owner)** — 09 §6.5: trigram only over titles, aliases, tags and the bodies of scripts `unicode61` cannot split into words, word beginnings through the word table (`MatchReason` `prefix`), three contentless FTS5 tables and one copy of the text; memory index schema 4, then 5 — a disposable cache rebuilt from the notes, no migration. Measured against a same-day build of the commit before: the 100K cache 1,065.5 → 366.6 MiB and search p95 154 → 48.6 ms; the retrieval proxy loses one row of its eight totals (tq-0032, the lead's ruling above). | `181c1d86`, `af7a1414` (the design, measured on a prototype); `457c1122` (the build, schema 4); `2684af55` (repair round 1, schema 5); `7076f653` (round 2); `13b7a3f9`, `c3fb1cf8` (pins) |

### Owner decisions taken — `feat/markdown-memory`, 2026-09-28 (the store revision)

On 2026-09-28 the owner asked for the unreleased migration chain to be combined into one `001` baseline, with new migrations starting from `002`, and then for the store "to start at 1 or 2, which is matching the migration table naming" (**SQ-1**); the same day, that four other never-distributed compatibility paths go (**SQ-2**: the legacy-root diagnostic, the home-README refresh, the pre-D2 `state/assets/` tolerance and the `.layout` backfills — slices S3–S6, built the same day; its row follows SQ-1's since rev 3.15). `tasks/store-revision/design.md` is the design of record; SQ-1a (the refusal's text), SQ-3 (provenance comments stay as history) and SQ-4 (the on-disk layout is unchanged) are the lead's rulings beside SQ-1. Like the tables above, these rows **are** the shipped behaviour.

| # | Question | Decision | Applied by |
|---|---|---|---|
| **SQ-1** | Which number does the schema start at once the development chain — the memory redesign's baseline 43 and Learning v2's 044/045 — is squashed again? | **The migration file's number (owner).** `001_baseline.sql` registers **1** and holds the whole schema, 044's and 045's columns and `learning_cursor` included (nothing seeds a cursor: a session with no row reads as the zero cursor); the next migration is `002_<name>.sql`, registering 2, and `BASELINE_VERSION` stays the first entry's version. No lineage marker and no compatibility path: a pre-squash database (42, or 43–45) reads as a version above this build's and meets the one refusal left — the 42 and 1..41 arms are deleted — whose text (SQ-1a, the lead's ruling) names both builds that could have written the file and `openalpaca config reset --factory`. The schema-42 fixture, its test and invariant fixture 05 are deleted. A pre-squash build refuses the new store as a legacy schema and tells the user to delete it, so the switch-over note says never to start one against it. | `62cbf02d` (S1: the baseline, the registry, the refusal, the tests and fixtures, `docs/api/database/`); S2, the documentation commit that records this row (the manuals, `CLAUDE.md`, the memory-redesign records) |
| **SQ-2** | Which of the other never-distributed compatibility paths go? Each reverses or moots an earlier decision. | **All four (owner).** (a) The legacy-root diagnostic: `store/legacy_root.rs` and its tests are deleted, with its callers in the daemon's boot and `store::open_home_database()` (which now only makes `state/` at 0700 and opens) and the `HOME`/`XDG_DATA_HOME` halves of the two test sandboxes — no older data directory is classified, warned about or refused over any more; D-D's diagnostic half is reversed and **T28** and **T31** are moot. (b) The home-README refresh: `README.md` is written only when absent, and the six superseded texts and their digest tripwire are gone. (c) The pre-D2 `state/assets/` tolerance: the test that planted such a row and the upload store's comments that promised such rows are left in place and answer dedup are deleted (one comment in `apps/openalpacad/src/routes/files_types.rs` still describes them); the audit of every `file_assets` writer kept `rel_path` and `project_root` nullable (a home-store row has no `project_root` by design, and `FileAssetRepository::insert`, a test-only writer, sets neither). (d) The `.layout` backfills: nothing adds `install_id` or `project_root` to a marker that exists; a fresh marker is written as before, and an unreadable line 1 is still repaired. The switch-over note in `docs/Installation_Manual.md` says what (b) and (d) leave. | `843651d1` (S3, (a)), `eee67d62` (S4, (b)), `f68b5f91` (S5, (c)), `c8b7e5a7` (S6, (d)); this row by the records pass (rev 3.15) |

Beside them, eq-017's gold is corrected (`c6db079f`), and no write-path target at 30K is set. Each row names the commits that applied it; like the tables above, these **are** the shipped behaviour.

|---|---|---|---|

| **LQ7** | The evaluation's L-S system seeds four gold notes present-tense, with no `valid_to`. Evaluation only. | **Kept (owner, the recommendation).** | No new code: L7 `b5c942e7` |
| **LQ8** | Index lines in the per-turn recall carry the full UUID, because `memory_read` takes full UUIDs; the alternative was `memory_read` accepting a unique ≥ 8-hex prefix. | **Kept as shipped (owner, the recommendation).** | No new code: L10b `c812dfbb` |
| **LQ9** | Automatic recall leaves out only conversation summaries (a pinned one is delivered), and forgetting a summary suppresses no source. | **Kept as shipped (owner, the recommendation).** | No new code: L10c `df835d96`, `5da63a2e` |
| **LQ10** | A model tool's edit or archive of a summary keeps origin `extracted_other`; a model-added `fact_key` on one was ignored by learning but still grouped by key (a GUI "conflict" tag, a `doctor` `same_fact_key` pair). | **The narrow refusal (owner, the recommendation):** an edit by anyone but the user — a tool call or the extractor — that sets or changes a conversation summary's `fact_key` is `authority_conflict` and writes nothing, and its message names the Memory view and `openalpaca memory edit`. The user's own edit (which makes the note the user's) and an edit that leaves the key alone are as before, so a model's rewording still leaves the note a summary. | `dc9cce84` (`Partition::update`, `crates/openalpaca_storage/src/memory_files/partition.rs`), `8c298f60` (the message) |

### Owner decisions taken — `feat/markdown-memory`, 2026-09-28 (the continuity design — taken; built)

On 2026-09-28 the owner asked for chat compaction to work with the memory system so the assistant picks up where a conversation left off. The design of record is `tasks/memory-redesign/11-continuity-design.md` (rev 1.1): one **working state** per conversation on the session row, written by one summariser, delivered in the history layer. The owner took its ten questions the same day, all as recommended except **CQ-9**. **Nothing of it is built:** unlike the tables above, these rows are decisions, not shipped behaviour, and each lands with the slice of 11 §13 named in its last column. *(Rev 3.18: built `4567c6cf..0d4c46f0` — slices 1–7 on the branch `track-b`, `4567c6cf..86c8911c`, merged into `feat/markdown-memory` as `0d4c46f0`, each slice's rulings in 11's revision log (revs 1.2–1.6); fix round `0cc26c11..db256d2b` and its records commit, the lead's rulings F1–F7 on the evaluation's findings (12 rev 1; 11 rev 1.7). The rows below are unchanged.)*

| # | Question | Decision | To be applied by |
|---|---|---|---|
| **CQ-1** | Delete compaction's Phase-1 extraction call? | **Yes (owner, the recommendation):** learning already reads every row (MD-10), and nothing reads the extraction. | 11 §13 slice 3 |
| **CQ-2** | One writer for the three summarisers, the episode note a projection of the working state with no model call of its own, and `episode_max_per_day` and the `orchestrator_episode` label removed? | **Yes (owner, the recommendation):** MD-16's note keeps its shape, triggers, visibility and forget; only how its text is produced changes. *(Rev 3.19: the projection half — and with it the label's removal — reversed by CQ-11, below; `episode_max_per_day` stays removed.)* | slices 2 and 4 |
| **CQ-3** | One model key for every summariser call, `[orchestrator.costs] summary_model`, and `[execution.context] compaction_model` deleted? | **Yes (owner, the recommendation).** | slice 3 |
| **CQ-4** | The state block exempt from the assembly's head-trim, and after a compaction the next turn's window starting after the state's `through` (a 4-row verbatim floor)? | **Yes (owner, the recommendation).** | slice 2 |
| **CQ-5** | Carry the previous conversation's state into a new conversation on the same lane and project for its first `carry_over_turns` (3) user turns, with a per-session `inherit: false` opt-out? | **Yes (owner, the recommendation).** MD-16 is not reversed: no episode is delivered by recall. | slice 5 |
| **CQ-6** | Does the lead receive the chat's working state after its objective? | **Yes (owner, the recommendation).** | slice 5 |
| **CQ-7** | The state on the wire (`SessionView`, `done.working_state_updated`, `done.continued_from`), a GUI card and line and a CLI line? | **Yes (owner, the recommendation).** | slice 6 |
| **CQ-8** | Does the session log's `compaction` record carry the full state, and replay splice it in? | **Yes (owner, the recommendation).** | slice 3 |
| **CQ-9** | Where does the schema change go: an edit of `001_baseline.sql`, or `002_working_state.sql`? | **`002_working_state.sql` (owner; the recommendation was editing 001):** the squash's baseline stays frozen, and 002 rebuilds `session` with the new columns. | slice 1 |
| **CQ-10** | Does the episode body read goal, done, decisions, open and next from the projection? | **Yes (owner, the recommendation),** gated: the episode split stays at ≥ 17/20 after slice 4, or the rendering is revised before release. *(Rev 3.19: the gate failed — 14, then 11 of 20 — and CQ-11, below, restored the summary's own call.)* | slice 4 |

### Owner decisions taken — `feat/markdown-memory`, 2026-09-30 (CQ-11 — taken; built)

After the continuity evaluation's re-runs (`tasks/memory-redesign/12-continuity-evaluation.md` rev 2: the projected episode note answered 11 of the episode split's 20 questions, against CQ-10's gate of 17 and the 17 the episode's own call answered in L8, 10 §5), the owner chose how the episode note is produced. The design of record is `tasks/memory-redesign/11-continuity-design.md` rev 1.9 (§3.4, §7, and the revision log's rev 1.9 entry for every ruling as built).

| # | Decision | Commits |
|---|---|---|

The working state and its refresh at the close are unchanged (the close still refreshes the state when ≥ 1 user turn is uncovered); `episode_max_per_day` stays deleted; `asked` stays in the state. Built with one consequence the decision did not name: the previous conversation's heading names the note by its id alone, since the note's title no longer carries the state's topic (11 rev 1.9 item 7). ### Owner decisions taken — `feat/markdown-memory`, 2026-10-02 (Round G: FW-1 — taken; built)

After draft PR #34 opened, the owner re-tested uploads and artifact generation through the app and asked for five things (Round G; the specs were written to a session scratchpad and are not tracked — `CLAUDE.md`'s *Round G* paragraph and the commits are the record): the approval card that came back after it was answered, fixed (`8a9d0c82`, `5c412dd5`, `ff3ca8cc`); Open and Reveal in Finder for artifacts (`01f133c9`, `74fd06b9`); a conversation named from the user's first message (`5211dd0f`, `51da88ee`, `2927e869`); two small fixes (`d6110da7`, `b6c79c4b`); and one decision, below. Like the tables above, this row **is** the shipped behaviour.

| # | Decision | Commits |
|---|---|---|
| **FW-1** | **An agent's files go to a centralized artifact store** (the owner: "there should be a centralized location for the artifacts like ~/.openalpaca/artifacts"). The re-test had found that `file_write` with a relative path in a conversation with no project wrote into the daemon's starting directory — in the packaged app the home store root, where the file was owned by no content kind and listed nowhere. `file_write` is now an artifact write unless the turn carries a project **and** the tools' root is not the home store: the same store path as `artifact_write` (the project's store when the turn carries one, else the home store), named after the path, its kind from the extension, the path as written kept as its summary, the same path again in one run its next version. The lead's rulings beside it: the name takes an 8-hex hash of the path when the grammar cannot carry the path whole; text whose extension no kind admits is `code`, stored `.bin`; two carried paths that differ only in case or in non-alphanumeric characters are one artifact's versions (stated, not closed in code); and — after the first live check saw agents read the result as a failure and write the file again with shell commands — the result says the write is complete, which absolute path to tell the user, never to copy it with the shell, and the relative path `file_read` opens it by when there is one. `file_read`, the lister and the shell are unchanged (FW-2 — *since rev 3.21* taken by the storage design as ST-10…ST-12, to be built in its Stage 2; *since rev 3.24* built as that Stage 2, `cfebde4c..d673478e`). | `6b17353c`, `1645eee5`, `43e879c9` (the routing, the name, the kind); `f31d182e`, `1464058f` (the result); `45cef1d8` (the Library draws the path as written); `6b217c59` (the collision wording) |

### Owner decisions taken — `feat/markdown-memory`, 2026-10-05 (the storage design: ST-1…ST-23; T34, T36 and FW-2 taken; Stages 0, 1 and 2 built)

On 2026-10-04 the owner asked that everything OpenAlpaca writes move under `~/.openalpaca/`. The storage design took **ST-1…ST-13** that day and **ST-14…ST-22** on 2026-10-05 and was approved at rev 0.6; its §0.1 holds the 23 rows (ST-23 since 2026-10-09), and they are not restated here (`tasks/storage-design/design.md`, rev 1.0; ADR-047). It is built in three stages, each from a plan the owner approves first. **Stage 0** — four fixes that need no design, from `tasks/storage-design/plan-stage0.md` (approved 2026-10-06) — was built on 2026-10-06…07 as `931460a4..a9dbed4e`: **F1**, T34 below; **F2**, the lead reads the head and the tail of each subagent's answer within the loop's inline bound instead of its first 500 characters, and a failed answer is kept whole (`bbfc676e`, `bc9b7d71`, `edacc6c2`, `f7259605`, `7f16eba6`; the final review's `fe7df469`); **F3**, the master key leaves the daemon's environment — installed in process, `OPENALPACA_MASTER_KEY` removed, so no program the daemon spawns receives it (`0047cf90`, `53ff0da8`, `f3980cba`; `33feb142`); **F4**, `queue_followup` (the lead's too), `steer_workflow` and `/steer` store the turn's resolved project root (`0d9a9293`, `8ba19fe2`, `29558d7a`, `85c9cacc`; `09b40624`). Every per-fix review was clean. Of the final review's findings, the one Important (the lead's prompt overstated what `check_subagent_status` returns for a failed subagent) was fixed in the wave `fe7df469`, `33feb142`, `09b40624`, which also took the per-fix reviews' deferred minors; M-1 to M-3 were fixed as doc lines in the records commit `0d7b5b27`; and M-4 is recorded beside T18. Gate at `09b40624`: `cargo test --workspace --exclude openalpaca_gui` 4,517 passed, 0 failed, 16 ignored; clippy 80, the baseline; `gen_api_docs.py --check` clean; `cargo check -p openalpaca_gui` clean; the bun gates not run (no file under `apps/openalpaca-gui` changed). The live check, through the daemon's HTTP API on an isolated store under `loopback-only.sb`, passed all four (`tasks/storage-design/stage0-live-report.md`, `a9dbed4e`). **Stage 1** — every project's content in its folder under `~/.openalpaca/projects/` with a permanent id, the resolver and registration, memory keyed by the project id, the re-point and the delete — was built on 2026-10-07…08 as `b8b768b6^..44d53783`, from `tasks/storage-design/plan-stage1.md` (approved 2026-10-07), with a whole-stage review whose fix wave and residual pass are `b923c81e..909e724c`; gate at `00f057fe` (4,722 passed, 0 failed, 16 ignored; clippy 78, the baseline since its S5a deleted two of the 80; the four bun gates green); live check `tasks/storage-design/stage1-live-report.md` (`44d53783`, all eight checks PASS; the app's surfaces proven by their vitest suites). It raised **T36** and the question **ST-23** answers, both answered on 2026-10-09 (below). **Stage 2** — agents work in the project's own folder: a working folder per turn (the project, or the conversation's scratch folder), real files with pointer rows, `file_edit`, `file_list`, `artifact_read`, the approval cards naming the folder, the kill of a timed-out command — was built on 2026-10-10 as `cfebde4c..d673478e` (172 commits; the whole-stage review's fix wave `6bc7042f..3810d9d8` among them), from `tasks/storage-design/plan-stage2.md` (approved 2026-10-10); gate at `3810d9d8` (5,080 passed, 0 failed, 16 ignored; clippy 78; the four bun gates green, 1,551 tests in 121 files); live check `tasks/storage-design/stage2-live-report.md` (`d673478e`; (a)–(h) and (j) PASS, (b2) the CLI keeps the preview's line breaks, 0 ERROR lines, the repository changed by the one approved file only, (i) not exercised live). It built FW-2 and, first, ST-23 (its slice S0; both below). The design's Part II lists the refusals its final wave added. A row below is the shipped behaviour only where its Commits column names commits.

| # | Decision | Commits |
|---|---|---|
| **ST-1…ST-22** | **Everything OpenAlpaca writes lives under `~/.openalpaca/`**: each project's content under `~/.openalpaca/projects/<dashed path>/` with a permanent id and nothing written inside the project's folder; with a project, agents read, list, edit, write and run commands in its folder with approval prompts, what a job is asked to produce is a real file and an artifact is a by-product; the shell is not confined, accepted for this version with every command asking first (ST-14). The rows are the design's §0.1; §17 names the records each supersedes (MD-5's marker half, MD-7, LQ5). | Stage 0 `931460a4..a9dbed4e`; Stage 1 `b8b768b6^..44d53783`; Stage 2 `cfebde4c..d673478e` |
| **T34** | (from the pending table, raised by Round G's live check) **The shipped `system_agent` template prompts like every other agent** — ST-14 made the fix mandatory and ruling R30 made it as T34's row recommended: `config/agents/system_agent.md` loses its two-line `require_confirmation_for` (its one entry, `file_delete`, names no tool), so the set derived from the tools' `destructive_hint` applies to that agent; the rule that an explicit list wins stays (`effective_confirmation_set`, `crates/openalpaca_core/src/security/sandbox/mod.rs:765` — since the storage design's Stage 2 a list naming `file_write` also asks for `file_edit`, R-T35); a test pins the shipped template's effective set; a store seeded earlier keeps its old template, and the switch-over note's step 7 names the two lines to delete, on a store switched over earlier too. Checked live: `system_agent`'s `uname -a` raised a confirmation before it ran. | `b79adc26` (the template and the test), `9196d69a` (the switch-over note) |
| **FW-2** | (from the pending table, raised by Round G's re-test) **The file tools use the turn's project** — taken as ST-10…ST-12 and built as the storage design's Stage 2: `file_read`, `file_list`, `file_write`, `file_edit`, `send` and `shell_execute` act in the turn's working folder (`tools::working_folder`: the project's root, or the conversation's scratch folder under `~/.openalpaca/scratch/`), never the daemon's starting directory; in a project `file_write` writes the real file with a pointer row and the old content kept; `artifact_read` reads a by-product back. Checked live: (a)–(h) and (j) PASS, (b2) the CLI keeps the preview's line breaks, 0 ERROR lines, the repository changed by the one approved file only, (i) not exercised live. | `cfebde4c..d673478e` (the plan `tasks/storage-design/plan-stage2.md`; the live check `d673478e`) |
| **T36** | (from the pending table, raised by the storage design's Stage 1; answered 2026-10-09) **No change**: `PATCH /v1/workspaces` with an `old_path` inside a project goes on re-pointing the whole project (the route resolves the path up to its project's root), while the delete refuses that shape with `422 WORKSPACE_NOT_A_ROOT` naming the root (R-S8). The design's §9 `PATCH` row stands; the lead had recommended the refusal. `tasks/storage-design/design.md` §0.2, rev 0.10. | — (nothing to build) |
| **ST-23** | (2026-10-09, the owner's answer to Stage 1's question on ST-7's `missing`) **A project whose recorded root no longer leads to its own folder reads missing** — a folder renamed by case only, or moved with a link left at the old path — like one whose folder is gone, and the user re-points it. `missing` becomes "the recorded root is not a directory, or no longer canonicalizes to itself", and a recorded root is matched as written before it is resolved wherever the daemon looks a project up (closing the design's §18 *review R1's deeper cross-wiring*). `tasks/storage-design/design.md` §0.1, §0.2, rev 0.10. | `cfebde4c..c60ecbc1` (the storage design's Stage 2, its slice S0, built first), with the fix wave's `a034ded6` (the CLI's `memory --project` and `sessions --workspace` send a recorded root as written) and `2dab8ce5` (a recorded root also matched as named, R-T29) |

### Owner decisions taken — `chore/toolchain-and-deps`, 2026-10-11/12/17 (the toolchain, dependency and modernization programme: DU-1…DU-32, DU-M1…M6, DU-D1…D4, DU-R1…R5, DU-W1…W7 taken on 2026-10-11, M01–M03 built; DU-33 and the no-compromise re-answers on 2026-10-12, rev 3.27; the 2026-10-17 review's D1–D3 on 2026-10-17, rev 3.29; every other pending decision of the programme but D8 by the owner's blanket answer of 2026-10-17 (PDT; 2026-10-18T01:56Z), rev 3.30; D8 by the owner's answer of 2026-10-19 (PDT; 2026-10-20T00:26Z), rev 3.33 — none built)

On 2026-09-25 the owner asked for a plan to bump Rust and every package to the latest stable version; on 2026-10-10, after three audits found the plan stale, to revise it and make the apps more flexible; on 2026-10-11 to embrace the dependencies' new APIs and features, remove every deprecated usage and stop `target/` growing to ~150 GB — and that day removed CLAUDE.md's "Simplicity First" rule, since this work needs major modification. The decisions are not restated here: Part I (the dependency wave, DU-1…DU-26) and Part II (the flexible apps, DU-27…DU-32) are `tasks/deps-upgrade/plan-rev3.md` §4 (rev 3.4); Part III (deprecated usage, new APIs and features, the target directory — DU-M1…M6, DU-D1…D4, DU-R1…R5, DU-W1…W7) is `tasks/deps-upgrade/modernization-plan.md` §5 (rev 1.2). Every decision was taken **as recommended**, with four answers of the owner's own: **DU-1 → `main`** (PR #34 merged as `f253cc26` on 2026-10-12 UTC; the branch `chore/toolchain-and-deps` is cut from it), **DU-2 → Rust 1.99.0**, **DU-13 → Bun 1.4.2**, and the rider that a change replacing an old API prefers the dependency's **latest** API form. One sequencing rule of the owner's: **nothing builds or tests before the cache behaviour is changed**, so Part III's target-directory commits opened the programme.

### Owner decisions taken — `chore/toolchain-and-deps`, 2026-10-17 (PDT; 2026-10-18T01:56Z) (the owner's blanket answer: the pending table's rows and their extension-design twins, each as its recommendation; none built)

On 2026-10-17 (PDT; 2026-10-18T01:56Z) the owner answered, in chat: "for the decisions all use recommend by Zcode or Recommend options". Read as written: every pending owner decision is taken as its current recommendation — the ZCode audit's where it gave or changed one (`tasks/pending-decisions.md` rev 5's *ZCode* lines; here T6(a), T7, T8, T13, T18, T22, T24 and T35), otherwise the one its source records — a conditional recommendation with its condition, and a recommendation that names a sub-choice whole; a row with no recommendation recorded stays pending. **Taking a decision builds nothing**: a row whose answer is no change stays as shipped, every other row's work waits for the commit that builds it, and until then the shipped code is what runs. Each row names its option — the source's label, or, where the source labels none, a number, (1), (2), …, in the order the decision sheet of 2026-10-17 lists the options (`tasks/decision-sheet-2026-10-17.md`, a working index) — spelled out in the row with the alternative it leaves where one matters, so no row depends on the sheet; and it names where its work is planned — an entry of `tasks/deps-upgrade/plan-rev3.md` by name, or "needs a plan entry" where no plan schedules it. The rows' full text as they stood pending is rev 3.29 of this file (`git show 476f1f81:tasks/api-fix-plan.md`) and `tasks/pending-decisions.md` (rev 6). T6(a), T6(c), T7 and T8 are the extension design's twins with no row of their own in the pending table (`tasks/extension-enable-design.md` §13 Q8–Q11); they are recorded here because this section is the record of owner decisions. **Taken as their recommendations, with nothing to build, outside the 28:** T14 and T31, moot in fact — GAP-22 was never built and C7 deleted the six `plugin_*` variants it would have stamped; nothing reads, writes or sweeps `state/assets/` since SQ-2 — and each recommendation (drop GAP-22; leave `state/assets/` unswept) is what the tree already does. **T2, T5 and T6(b)** — lessons §6 tensions with no row in the pending table, applied as written by the extension design the owner accepted at rev 15 (a bundled sub-unit of a plugin is a contribution, never its own row; a disable tears down live; no automatic retry ladder for a failed server) — are taken by the same answer as lessons §6 recommends them, which is what that design applied: nothing to build, outside this subsection's 28 and the rev's count (`tasks/pending-decisions.md` §1, *Closed without a decision*, which records all five so). **Left pending:** T6(a)'s sub-choice, the one row of the pending table above. *(Taken 2026-10-19 (PDT; 2026-10-20T00:26Z) — the thirteenth subsection below.)* **The options a settled rule excludes** — `tasks/pending-decisions.md` §6, 28 options — each kept the rule: option not taken (2026-10-17); §6-23, the overlay title bar, is (c)-5's option (b), not taken with (c)-5 (a). The programme's decisions taken by the same answer are the toolchain subsection's second table, above.

| # | Decision taken (option) | Work it schedules, and where | Commits |
|---|---|---|---|
| **T1** (ext design §13 Q5) | **(1) No** per-tool deny rule set: the per-agent ALLOW axis stays the only way to narrow tools, under the two-axis rule and C8's purge; were one ever taken, deny would be evaluated before every allow path and mode shortcut. | None to build. The rejection is recorded as ext design §11.2 finding (4), written in that design's rev 18. | — (no change) |

The once-per-lane log of a request crossing 10 % of its window, which ext design §13 Q6's wording of the recommendation names, does not exist and needs a plan entry. | — (a rule) |

On 2026-10-19 (PDT; 2026-10-20T00:26Z) the owner answered, in chat: "all use you opinion for the open rows, use the best choice, prefer using claude code's design, use Zcode design when claude code's design is not available". Read as a rule, applied row by row: **Claude Code's design where one exists; else ZCode's; else the lead's recommendation** — each row's *(decided by: …)* names which of the three decided it. The rows are the ones the research pass of 2026-10-19 covered: fourteen rows of `tasks/pending-decisions.md` §1's list — among them every decision this plan's §0 still had pending, T6(a)'s sub-choice (the pending table above) and D8 (the toolchain subsection), with the parts left pending beneath three taken rows (B-11's cap value, App PATH's credential-pointer keys, and B-21's companion, the `agent.config` drop) — and the owner's proposal of the same day for the subagent brief, **SA-1…SA-4**, which the lead read as four separable changes and the answer takes. The rest of `tasks/pending-decisions.md` §1's list stays pending, unchanged (its rev 9; the day's decision sheet, `tasks/decision-sheet-2026-10-19.md`, is a local working view the `*.md` rule leaves untracked). The sources: "Claude Code (this session's contracts)" is the lead's session's own tool contracts, read directly; "Claude Code (secondhand)" is ZCode's account of that design and the audit's T6(a) row, never cited as Claude Code behaviour on its own; `ZCode:<path>:<line>` is the repository at `aac47556`, read on 2026-10-19 by four readers each followed by a citation verifier, the code winning where a ZCode doc disagreed; and "the lead" is the lead's recommendation given in chat before the answer. **Taking a decision builds nothing**: the tree's behaviour is unchanged until a commit builds the answer. Each commit is placed after P38, in `modernization-plan.md` §6.3's merged order, as an entry of plan-rev3 rev 3.18's new §5.5 (*Commits from the 2026-10-19 answers (no version moves)*), each written as §5.4's entries are — except N-MCP-1, whose existing entry is amended in place (after G-base's run of record, before P19), Q06, a Part II commit rewritten, and `agent.config`'s drop, which rides migration 005 with B-21's change; the beta's scope is scheduling only; SA-1, SA-3 and SA-4 wait for a design to write first, `tasks/subagent-brief-design.md`, scheduled after P38 and before any of their commits.
